\section*{Summary of the two hundred fifty-five conjectures}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Summary of the conjectures}

\paragraph{Part I: conjectures from the local--global random model.}

\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Prime-power contamination calculus
(Conjecture~\ref{c21}).}  For a balanced pair race $(n,n+d)$ mod
$m$, the surviving prime-square orientations
$(q^2-d,q^2)$, $(q^2,q^2+d)$ occupy provably computable residue
classes, and the operator
$\mathcal C_{d,m}(a;x)=\sum_{o}\sum_{q}\Lambda(q^2)\Lambda(\text{prime
member})$ determines the drift vector: for twins,
$\M_x\bigl(D_1(t)\log^2t/\sqrt t\bigr)\to c_T=\tfrac12C(x,x^2-2)$
(mod $5$) and $\to2c_T$ on class $7$ (mod $8$), symmetric
differences $\to0$; in general each class deficit satisfies
$\M_x(\mathrm{deficit}\cdot\log^2t/\sqrt t)\to\lim\mathcal
C_{d,m}(a;x)/\sqrt x$.  Convergence of these logarithmic means at
drift scale is part of the conjecture (Rubinstein--Sarnak-type
structure for pattern races).

\item \textbf{Pinned singular-series variance (Conjecture~\ref{c12}).}
With $R(h)=C_4(0,2,h,h+2)/\SG(2)^2$ and
$G(H)=\sum_{h\le H}(1-h/H)(R(h)-1)$: (i) [conditional proposition]
the moving-window twin-pair count obeys
$\operatorname{Var}/\mathbb E=1+\SG(2)(2G(H)-1)/\log^2x
+o(\log^{-2}x)$; (ii) $-G(H)/\log H\to\infty$: pair counts are more
sub-Poisson than prime counts.

\item \textbf{Least-prime ordering deficit (Conjecture~\ref{c22}).}
With $U(a,q)=\Li(p(a,q))/\varphi(q)$: (i) $U\Rightarrow\mathrm{Exp}(1)$
marginally (attributed), Gumbel maximum under joint independence;
(ii) the dyadic averages of $\Theta(q)=(1-\mathbb E_a[U])\log q$
over prime $q\in[Q,2Q]$ converge to a limit $\Theta>0$: the ordered
primes fill residue classes faster than any exchangeable model, in
excess of the derived discreteness and injectivity baselines.

\item \textbf{Goldbach lane race (Conjecture~\ref{c25}).}  For
$n\equiv2\pmod4$, prime squares enter ordered Goldbach
representations only through the $(1,1)$ lane mod $4$, so with
$D=R_3-R_1$ and the explicit weighted census $D_{\mathrm{sys}}$:
$\mathbb E_x[D-D_{\mathrm{sys}}]=o(\mathbb E_x[D_{\mathrm{sys}}])$
under logarithmic sampling; the drift vanishes on the internal null
subprogression $n\equiv10\pmod{12}$; the sign density of $D$ tends
to $\tfrac12$.

\item \textbf{Least Goldbach summand (Conjecture~\ref{c15}).}  With
$s(n)$ the least prime $p\nmid n$ with $n-p$ prime,
$U(n)=\SG(n)\sum_{p\le s(n),\,p\nmid n}1/\log(n-p)$, and the dyadic-log
ensemble $\mathbb E_X$: (i) $U\Rightarrow\mathrm{Exp}(1)$; (ii)
$\Theta_G(X)=(1-\mathbb E_XU)\log X\to\Theta_G>0$, the Goldbach
sibling of the ordering deficit.

\item \textbf{Polynomial--exponential entanglement
(Conjecture~\ref{c11}).}  Primes $n^2+2^n$ force $n\equiv3\pmod6$;
with $\kappa_S=3\,D_S/\prod_{p\in S}(1-1/p)$ built from the
CRT-exact survivor density $D_S$ over the joint period of $S$ and
the lane bonus $3$ at the primes $2$ and $3$: $\kappa_{S_z}$
converges along the canonical
exhaustion $S_z=\{5\le p\le z\}$ to $\kappa$, the counting function
is $\sim\sum_{n\equiv3(6),\,n\le N}\kappa/\log(n^2+2^n)\asymp\log
N$; whether the limit factors exactly (as observed through $p\le19$)
is an open question.

\item \textbf{Multibase Fermat quotients (Conjecture~\ref{c23}).}
For $p\nmid a$: the Eisenstein--Lerch homomorphism confines
multiplicatively dependent bases to a rational subtorus, and
(iv) for multiplicatively independent bases
$(q_p(a_1),\dots,q_p(a_r))/p$ equidistributes on the full torus as
$p$ varies; (v) at most finitely many simultaneous Wieferich primes;
plus single-base clauses: KS-discrepancy LIL at constant
$1/\sqrt2$, shrinking-target law
$\#\{p\le x:q_p<K\}\sim\sum\min(1,K/p)$, Wieferich-count LIL and an
almost-sure CLT at the $du/u$ weighting.

\item \textbf{Uniform de Polignac covariance field
(Conjecture~\ref{c16}).}  $\pi_d(x)=\SG(d)\Li_2(x)(1+o(1))$
uniformly for even $d\le(\log x)^A$; the moving-window residual
field has covariance
$K(d,d')H/\log^3x+(1/\log^4x)\sum_{|h|\le H}(H-|h|)K_4(d,d';h)$,
triple term dominant on the polylogarithmic window class, with
Poisson/Gaussian regimes as finite-$x$ corrections to independence.

\item \textbf{Race sub-diffusivity (Conjecture~\ref{c18}).}
Balanced-race steps at event index have negative autocorrelations
obeying $\rho_k(x)=-(c_k\log\log x+d_k)/\log x\,(1+o(1))$; under
uniformity in $k$, tail summability, and $\sum c_k,\sum|d_k|<\infty$,
the diffusivity is
$\sigma^2(x)=1-2(\sum c_k\log\log x+\sum d_k)/\log x\,(1+o(1))$,
those hypotheses being model-layer, not asserted asymptotically;
the asymptotic diffusive-vs-rigid dichotomy is registered open,
with the rigid branch selected only at Conjecture~\ref{c21}(i-b)
for its contaminated races.

\item \textbf{Uniform quadratic de Polignac and its constants
(Conjecture~\ref{c1}).}  $\#\{n\le N:n^2+1,\,n^2+1+d
\text{ prime}\}=C(d)I_d(N)(1+o(1))$ uniformly for even
$d\le(\log N)^B$; a separate power-saving rate clause; and the
family law: moments of $C(d)$ converge to derived Euler products
(mean $2.7456\ldots$, sd $1.6840\ldots$), the empirical law
converging to the random product with CRT-independent local factors.

\item \textbf{First occurrences of prime gaps
(Conjecture~\ref{c19}).}  On realized gaps: $\log p(g)/\sqrt g$
bounded between positive constants;
$\liminf=\sqrt{\e^{\gamma}/2}$ conditional on the realization
hypothesis; and
$\log p(g)=\sqrt g+\tfrac12\log g-\tfrac12\log\SG^*(g)+E_g$ with
$\Pr[E_g\le t]\to1-\exp(-\e^{2(t-\mu)})$, the min-type Gumbel at
scale $\tfrac12$ forced by the slope-$2$ hazard expansion.

\item \textbf{Stern lane race (Conjecture~\ref{c14}).}  In
$n=p+2k^2$, square contamination $n=q^2+2k^2$ sits in the $k$-even
lane iff $n\equiv1\pmod8$, $k$-odd iff $n\equiv3$, and is
impossible for $n\equiv5,7$ (provable null classes); on contaminated
classes the clean-minus-contaminated difference obeys
$\mathbb E_X[D-D_{\mathrm{sys}}]=o(\mathbb E_X[D_{\mathrm{sys}}])$
with the $\Lambda(q^2)$-weighted census $D_{\mathrm{sys}}$.

\item \textbf{Microscopic variance law (Conjecture~\ref{c20}).}
[Conditional on quantitative Hardy--Littlewood]  For windows of
length $H=\lambda(\log x)^{\alpha}$,
$\operatorname{Var}X/\mathbb EX=1-(\log H+\gamma+\log2\pi-1)/\log x
+o(1/\log x)$ uniformly in $1\le\alpha\le A$, with Gallagher's
Poisson law at the boundary $\alpha=1$.

\item \textbf{Null-mechanism race (Conjecture~\ref{c8}).}  For
$(n^2+1,n^2+3)$, square contamination is algebraically impossible (single bounded exception $(n,q)=(1,2)$);
the race mod $5$ is driftless at the contamination scale,
$\M_x(D(t)\log^2t/\sqrt t)\to0$---the negative control for
item 1---and the logarithmic occupation of leadership tends to
$\tfrac12$ with Gaussian fluctuations $\sqrt{\log2/\log x}$,
conjectured directly (the event-index invariance principle is a
separately falsifiable local hypothesis, which does not imply it).

\item \textbf{Cubic-shift constants (Conjecture~\ref{c2}).}  For
non-cube $a$, the three-state local law gives
$\mathbb E_a[\omega(p)]=1$ exactly, so the limit law of $C(a)$ has
mean exactly $1$ (an $L^2$-martingale argument), derived sd
$0.2762\ldots$; counts are uniform over non-cube
$a\le(\log N)^B$.

\item \textbf{Hardy--Littlewood's Conjecture F as a family (Conjecture~\ref{c24}).}
$Q_A(N)=C(A)I_A(N)(1+o(1))$ uniformly over odd $A\le(\log N)^B$;
same-index window covariance
$[C(A,A')-C(A)C(A')]H/4\log^2N$ (negative for locally exclusive
pairs), with the off-index pinned sum at the same $1/4\log^2N$
order left open.

\item \textbf{Twin-member Goldbach, orientation-resolved
(Conjecture~\ref{c17}).}  Under $n$-uniform Hardy--Littlewood for
the four role systems,
$R_T(n)=[\sum_o\SG_4^{(o)}(n)]\int_5^{n-5}dt/(\log^2t\log^2(n-t))
\,(1+o(1))$, with the exact identities
$\SG_4^{(\mathrm{lu})}=\SG_4^{(\mathrm{ul})}$ and
$\SG_4^{(\mathrm{uu})}(n)=\SG_4^{(\mathrm{ll})}(n-4)$.

\item \textbf{Triplet contamination (Conjecture~\ref{c3}).}  For
$(n,n+2,n+6)$ mod $5$, the unique surviving square configuration is
the doubly-thinned $(q^2-2,q^2,q^2+4)$ (the other two configurations dying for every prime $q>3$), contaminating class
$2$: $\M_x\bigl((\pi_3(t;5,1)-\pi_3(t;5,2))\log^3t/\sqrt
t\bigr)\to c_3$, the Bateman--Horn constant of
$(q,q^2-2,q^2+4)$.

\item \textbf{Sexy-pair contamination matrix
(Conjecture~\ref{c7}).}  For $(n,n+6)$ both orientations survive on
complementary $q$-classes, feeding start classes $3$ and $1$ (mod
$5$ and mod $8$) with independent constants $c_A,c_B$ (the
$(q,q^2\mp6)$ Bateman--Horn constants) in the drift-scale sense;
class $2$ (mod $5$) and classes $5,7$ (mod $8$) are provably clean.

\item \textbf{Fibonacci--Lucas twins (Conjecture~\ref{c9}).}  The
odd prime-divisor pools of $F_p$ and $L_p$ are disjoint (ranks $p$
vs $2p$; $\gcd=2$ iff $3\mid p$); only finitely many $p$ have both
prime; the naive joint accounting assigns prior mass about $1.4\times10^{-2}$ to the index range beyond $10^4$, in which the catalogued index $148091$ falls, recorded
descriptively and conditionally on the probable-prime status of both
$F_{148091}$ and $L_{148091}$, and fatal to any completeness list.

\item \textbf{Factorial twins (Conjecture~\ref{c10}).}  Window
rigidity: $n!+a$ is composite for $2\le|a|\le n$ (theorem), so
$\pm1$ are the only bounded offsets; $n=3$ uniqueness is attributed;
the joint-fluctuation clause is a declared-model CLT for
$F_+-F_-$ under explicit covariance and higher-cumulant
hypotheses.

\item \textbf{Boundary trichotomy (Conjecture~\ref{c13}).}  For
$\deg F\ge2$ with positive leading coefficient, $F(m)-F(j)$ prime
forces $m-j=1$ outside a bounded region; for $x^3+cx$ the boundary
lane $3m^2-3m+1+c$ is dead-parity for odd $c$, dead-$3$-adic for
even $c\equiv2\pmod3$, admissible exactly for $c\equiv0,4\pmod6$,
with uniform Bateman--Horn counts over admissible
$c\le(\log M)^B$.

\item \textbf{Power-obstruction ladder (Conjecture~\ref{c4}).}
$m^k=p+j^k$ is impossible for composite $k$ (theorem); for prime
$k$ it forces $j=m-1$ and reduces to primality of the irreducible
$D_k(m)=m^k-(m-1)^k$, which follows Bateman--Horn uniformly over
the prime-$k$ lanes.

\item \textbf{Alternating cyclotomic chain (Conjecture~\ref{c6}).}
Infinitely many primes $p$ with $\Phi_3(p)$ and
$\Phi_6(\Phi_3(p))$ prime, at the derived Bateman--Horn rate;
alternation is the unique admissible continuation in the anchored
construction space.

\item \textbf{Twin cyclotomic bases (Conjecture~\ref{c5}).}  Of the
$\varphi(k)=2$ family, only $k=3$ survives ($k=4$ parity-dead,
$k=6$ a translate), and $\Phi_3(n)$, $\Phi_3(n+1)$ are
simultaneously prime infinitely often with the computed
Bateman--Horn constant.

\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part II: structural conjectures, in programme order.}

\begin{enumerate}
\setcounter{enumi}{25}
\item \textbf{Connected motif generating functional (Conjecture~\ref{s1}).}  A full connected diagram calculus for all joint cumulants of prime motifs.
\item \textbf{Complete overlap-renormalization filtration (Conjecture~\ref{s2}).}  A classification of every possible covariance scale and rigid eigenspace in a finite motif family.
\item \textbf{Regularized mesoscopic local--spectral trace formula (Conjecture~\ref{s3}).}  Independently mollified Euler-product and multiple-zero functionals agree after named counterterms, and the limit is regularization independent.
\item \textbf{Anchored arithmetic polymer expansion (Conjecture~\ref{s4}).}  Palm cluster activities are graded by the number of new prime constraints beyond one anchored motif.
\item \textbf{Topological expansion of non-Gaussianity (Conjecture~\ref{s5}).}  Each independent cycle in a connected diagram's overlap graph costs one logarithmic order, so the leading non-Gaussian odd-cumulant term is carried by the tree-like diagrams.
\item \textbf{Connected first-arrival functional (Conjecture~\ref{s6}).}  The entire least-prime point process has connected kernels obtained from same-class prime correlations.
\item \textbf{Tested Gauss-polyspectral reciprocity (Conjecture~\ref{s7}).}  Scalar tensor observables have exact Gauss-transport identities under the random-modulus law and dual arithmetic limits.
\item \textbf{Rubinstein--Sarnak terminal chaos dichotomy (Conjecture~\ref{s8}).}  Terminal extremes are directed by the limiting low-zero chaos measure, with an explicit self-averaging versus Cox criterion.
\item \textbf{Nonlinear spectral response calculus (Conjecture~\ref{s9}).}  Low-zero and exceptional-zero perturbations pass through first-arrival statistics by universal Volterra response operators.
\item \textbf{Local-information capacity of odd class groups (Conjecture~\ref{s10}).}  Cohen--Lenstra independence has a sharp two-resource boundary governed by cell entropy and conductor complexity.
\item \textbf{Kummer--Haar law on relation tori (Conjecture~\ref{s11}).}  Saturated multiplicative relations give the exact horizontal support and Haar law of Fermat-quotient vectors.
\item \textbf{Rank-dimensional finite-logarithm large sieve (Conjecture~\ref{s12}).}  A positive power range of frequencies obeys a large sieve whose dual dimension is saturated rank.
\item \textbf{Mesoscopic-to-lattice shrinking-target transition (Conjecture~\ref{s13}).}  Haar universality persists for growing targets, while bounded lattice targets acquire a separate arithmetic regulator intensity.
\item \textbf{Global-lattice $p$-adic regulator-matrix law (Conjecture~\ref{s14}).}  A fixed integral Galois-relation lattice controls horizontal regulator matrices and their determinantal rare events.
\item \textbf{Functorial horizontal logarithms on tori (Conjecture~\ref{s15}).}  A finitely generated global subgroup of a torus has a functorial matrix-valued finite-logarithm Haar law.
\item \textbf{Entropy--conductor profile of arboreal resolution (Conjecture~\ref{s16}).}  Exact class-measure entropy and Artin-conductor profiles determine observable depth and finite-index shifts.
\item \textbf{Arboreal family large sieve and cumulant independence (Conjecture~\ref{s17}).}  Growing preimage quotients satisfy square-root trace bounds and higher connected Frobenius factorization over parameters.
\item \textbf{Coloured dynatomic Galois-factor process (Conjecture~\ref{s18}).}  Exact-period components generate independent coloured, Frobenius-marked factor processes, with growing-degree support restrictions.
\item \textbf{Complexity-uniform primitive valuation process (Conjecture~\ref{s19}).}  The complete primitive valuation vector has an adelic product law under an explicit height--discriminant complexity regime and uniform squarefull tails.
\item \textbf{Divisor-sensitive dynamical gcd classification (Conjecture~\ref{s20}).}  Positive normalized gcd height is exactly eventual entry into a preperiodic subvariety carrying the relevant common divisor.
\item \textbf{Frobenius-marked Poisson--Dirichlet process (Conjecture~\ref{s21}).}  Macroscopic polynomial-value factors carry fixed-point-size-biased Galois marks.
\item \textbf{Three-scale polynomial factorization (Conjecture~\ref{s22}).}  Small valuations, mesoscopic scale-invariant factors, and macroscopic marked factors form one conservation-corrected product structure.
\item \textbf{Adelic Gibbs gluing for reducible values (Conjecture~\ref{s23}).}  Reducible polynomial factor processes are an archimedean--$p$-adic Gibbs mixture of independent component processes.
\item \textbf{Full multivariate adelic saddle law (Conjecture~\ref{s24}).}  Joint smoothness is governed by a complete joint Perron action, saddle displacement, and Hessian, not a scalar Euler correction.
\item \textbf{Buchstab--Bateman--Horn component flow (Conjecture~\ref{s25}).}  Rough prime, semiprime, and higher-almost-prime polynomial values follow universal Buchstab components with Galois marks.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part III: six conjectures across fields, in statement order.}
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{50}
\item \textbf{Relative polynomial pretentiousness inverse principle (Conjecture~\ref{t1}).} A persistently nonzero logarithmic correlation of completely multiplicative unimodular functions along multiplicatively independent irreducible polynomials forces one existential block of primitive characters and archimedean parameters with finite relative pretentious distances, the balance identity $\sum_j t_j \deg P_j = 0$, and a nonvanishing joint character average at a common modulus.
\item \textbf{Monotone threshold purification of vector paths (Conjecture~\ref{t2}).} Every monotone fractional activation schedule of $m$ vectors of norm at most one in $\R^d$ admits a rounding to distinct irreversible activation times whose path error is $O(\sqrt d)$ uniformly in $m$, containing the open Euclidean Steinitz prefix-sum problem and asserting removal of the logarithm from Banaszczyk's $O(\sqrt{d+\log m})$.
\item \textbf{Entropy dimension of random-free graphons (Conjecture~\ref{t3}).} If the row space of a $\{0,1\}$-valued graphon is Ahlfors $s$-regular then $H(G(n,W))/(n\log n)\to s$, the hypothesis class being nonvacuous at every $s\in(0,2]$ by Cantor-type threshold constructions.
\item \textbf{A Hessian principle for isolated microcanonical graph phases (Conjecture~\ref{t4}).} Under isolated-phase, transversally Lipschitz, and nonemptiness hypotheses, the tangent block averages of a microcanonical coloured graph ensemble fluctuate at scale $n^{-1}$ around their mean with centred Gaussian limit of covariance $A^{-1}$, the inverse of the contracted entropy Hessian.
\item \textbf{Sharp stability of entropy idempotence on finite abelian groups (Conjecture~\ref{t5}).} The squared total variation distance from a probability measure to the nearest uniform coset measure is at most $2/\log 2$ times the convolution entropy defect $H(\mu*\mu)-H(\mu)$, sharply, the constant pinned by discretized Gaussians on $\Z/p\Z$; some universal constant already follows from the Green--Manners--Tao entropic inverse theorem, so the sharp constant and the extremal geometry are the content.
\item \textbf{Stationary convolution-entropy rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{t6}).} An ergodic stationary process over a finite abelian group preserves entropy under independent self-convolution exactly when its quotient by the translational stabilizer has zero entropy, so in particular every ergodic binary process of intermediate entropy gains entropy under independent XOR.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part IV: twenty conjectures on the class-counting polynomial, in statement order.}
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{56}
\item \textbf{Irreducibility and connectivity (Conjecture~\ref{u1}).}  $H_G(U,Y)=C_G(1+U,Y)$ is irreducible in $\Z[U,Y]$ exactly when $G$ is connected, the disconnected direction being immediate from Rossmann's multiplicativity and the connected direction verified by exact factorization for all $996$ connected graphs on at most seven vertices.
\item \textbf{Recovery of the subgraph-component polynomial (Conjecture~\ref{u2}).}  Equality of class-counting polynomials forces equality of Tittmann--Averbouch--Makowsky subgraph-component polynomials, though the two arrays, $(|S|,|N[S]|-c(G[S]))$ against $(|S|,c(G[S]))$, admit no apparent functional relation.
\item \textbf{Two-field rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{u3}).}  The adjoint-rank distributions of the graphical Lie algebras over $\Fq_2$ and $\Fq_3$ together determine them over every finite field.
\item \textbf{Hybrid rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{u4}).}  The $\Fq_2$ rank distribution together with the subgraph-component polynomial determines the class-counting polynomial, while the degree sequence in place of $Q_G$ does not suffice.
\item \textbf{Binary edge-deck reconstruction (Conjecture~\ref{u5}).}  The multiset of binary specializations $C_{G-e}(2,Y)$ over the edges determines a graph with at least four edges up to isomorphism, a statement in the orbit of the edge reconstruction conjecture and the high-risk member of the part.
\item \textbf{Bipartite binary rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{u6}).}  Within bipartite graphs the single specialization $C_G(2,Y)$ determines $C_G$.
\item \textbf{Connected-pseudoforest binary rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{u7}, resolved false).}  The proposed one-field rigidity for trees and connected unicyclic graphs is refuted by an explicit seven-vertex unicyclic pair sharing the binary specialization with distinct degree multisets, the unique violating class through nine vertices, and the tree case survives exhaustively through fourteen vertices.
\item \textbf{Chordal binary rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{u8}).}  The same one-field rigidity holds within chordal graphs.
\item \textbf{Recovery of the domination number (Conjecture~\ref{u9}).}  Equality of class-counting polynomials forces equality of domination numbers, a statement strictly between Rossmann's connected-domination theorem and the false total-domination analogue.
\item \textbf{Recovery of the independent domination number (Conjecture~\ref{u10}).}  Equality of class-counting polynomials forces equality of independent domination numbers.
\item \textbf{Global double log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{u11}).}  For every graph the coefficient sequence $a$ of $f_G(1+Z)$ is log-concave and remains log-concave after one application of $L(a)_i=a_i^2-a_{i-1}a_{i+1}$, the third iterate of $L$ failing for a thirteen-vertex graph.
\item \textbf{Strict log-concavity for connected graphs (Conjecture~\ref{u12}).}  For connected graphs with at least one edge the inequality $a_i^2>a_{i-1}a_{i+1}$ is strict at every interior position of the support.
\item \textbf{Strict second-order log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{u13}).}  Under the same hypotheses $L(a)$ is strictly log-concave at every interior position of its support.
\item \textbf{Rank-slice strict log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{u14}).}  For connected graphs every non-monomial rank slice $[Y^r]F_G(1+Z,Y)$ has strictly log-concave coefficients at interior positions, the second-order strengthening of the slice statement failing for an eight-vertex graph.
\item \textbf{Interval support at fixed rank (Conjecture~\ref{u15}, resolved false).}  The proposed gaplessness is refuted by a connected eight-vertex graph whose support sizes attaining rank five skip size two, the unique violating order, while the transposed statement was already false.
\item \textbf{Coefficientwise tree extremality (Conjecture~\ref{u16}).}  Among $n$-vertex trees the shifted polynomial $f_T(1+Z)$ is sandwiched coefficientwise between path and star, with equality only at the extremes.
\item \textbf{Star rank-majorization (Conjecture~\ref{u17}).}  For $n\ge5$ and every prime power $q$ the decreasingly ordered adjoint-rank distribution of the star majorizes that of every $n$-vertex tree, the hypothesis being sharp since the star fails to majorize the path at $(n,q)=(4,2)$.
\item \textbf{Tree rank-variance extremality (Conjecture~\ref{u18}).}  For $n\ge4$ and every prime power the variance of $\rank(\operatorname{ad}_x)$ over a uniformly random element is uniquely minimized by the path and uniquely maximized by the star among $n$-vertex trees.
\item \textbf{Leaf-count rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{u19}).}  Equality in the lemma's bound $s(T)\ge n-\ell(T)+2$ holds exactly for paths and stars.
\item \textbf{Diameter rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{u20}).}  Equality in the lemma's bound $s(T)\ge\operatorname{diam}(T)+1$ holds exactly for paths and stars.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part V: twenty conjectures on pattern Lie algebras of posets, in statement order.}
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{76}
\item \textbf{Adjoint--Kirillov determination (Conjecture~\ref{v1}).}  Equality of adjoint enumerators $A_{P,q}$ forces equality of Kirillov enumerators $K_{P,q}$, the two contractions of the same bracket, verified across $64$ adjoint-collision classes.
\item \textbf{Lower-central recovery (Conjecture~\ref{v2}).}  The adjoint enumerator determines the vector of lower-central factor dimensions of $L_P$, which was constant on every adjoint-collision class in testing.
\item \textbf{Ordinary Poincar\'e recovery (Conjecture~\ref{v3}).}  The adjoint enumerator is conjectured to determine the ordinary cohomological Poincar\'e polynomial, tested only for $\dim H^2$ and $\dim H^3$ and which rests on the deposited cohomology data.
\item \textbf{Derivation recovery (Conjecture~\ref{v4}).}  The adjoint enumerator determines $\dim\operatorname{Der}(L_P)$, which was constant on every adjoint-collision class.
\item \textbf{Adjoint Poincar\'e recovery (Conjecture~\ref{v5}).}  The adjoint enumerator is conjectured to determine every adjoint-cohomology dimension $\dim H^i(L_P,L_P)$, tested only for $\dim H^1$, $\dim H^2$, and $\dim H^3$ from the deposited data.
\item \textbf{Centroid-dimension recovery (Conjecture~\ref{v6}).}  The adjoint enumerator determines $\dim\operatorname{Cent}(L_P)$, which was constant on every adjoint-collision class.
\item \textbf{Adjoint spectrum characteristic independence (Conjecture~\ref{v7}).}  The set of attained adjoint ranks is independent of $q$, agreeing across $\Fq_2$, $\Fq_3$, and $\Fq_5$ in every case.
\item \textbf{Kirillov spectrum characteristic independence (Conjecture~\ref{v8}).}  The set of attained Kirillov ranks is independent of $q$, agreeing across $\Fq_2$, $\Fq_3$, and $\Fq_5$ in every case.
\item \textbf{Two-field arithmetic rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{v9}).}  Equality of adjoint enumerators over $\Fq_2$ and $\Fq_3$ is conjectured to force equality over every field, tested only at the single holdout $q=5$ on $16$ three-field collision classes.
\item \textbf{Adjoint stochastic field monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{v10}).}  For $q<q'$ the adjoint-rank distribution over $\Fq_{q'}$ first-order stochastically dominates that over $\Fq_q$, held in all $221$ field-pair tests, while the likelihood-ratio strengthening is false.
\item \textbf{Kirillov likelihood-ratio field monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{v11}, resolved false).}  The proposed monotone likelihood-ratio domination of the normalized even-rank distributions is refuted by a seven-point poset whose likelihood ratio from $\Fq_2$ to $\Fq_3$ already decreases across the even ranks, while the first-order dominance of Conjecture~\ref{v10} stands.
\item \textbf{Centre-one reverse determination (Conjecture~\ref{v12}).}  When $\dim Z(L_P)=1$ the Kirillov enumerator determines the adjoint enumerator, verified on all $43$ centre-one records.
\item \textbf{Centre-one Laplace positivity (Conjecture~\ref{v13}).}  When $\dim Z(L_P)=1$ the incidence quotient $R_{P,q}$ has nonnegative integer coefficients, on all $43$ centre-one records.
\item \textbf{Centre-one quotient shape (Conjecture~\ref{v14}, resolved false).}  The proposed unimodality of $R_{P,q}$ under a line centre is refuted by a seven-point poset whose quotient has a strict interior valley, while the positivity and interval support of Conjecture~\ref{v13} stand.
\item \textbf{Hasse-forest reverse determination (Conjecture~\ref{v15}).}  When the Hasse cover graph is a forest the Kirillov enumerator determines the adjoint enumerator, across $44$ forest-collision classes.
\item \textbf{Hasse-forest coadjoint saturation (Conjecture~\ref{v16}).}  On a Hasse forest the Kirillov support is the full even range $\{0,2,\ldots,b_K\}$, filled in every case.
\item \textbf{Hasse-forest Kirillov unimodality (Conjecture~\ref{v17}).}  On a Hasse forest the even-rank sequence of $K_{P,q}$ is unimodal, strict log-concavity being false.
\item \textbf{Hasse-forest breadth--orbit bound (Conjecture~\ref{v18}).}  On a Hasse forest $b_K\le 2b_A$, the unrestricted bound failing fourteen times off the forest class.
\item \textbf{Unitriangular Kirillov strict log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{v19}).}  For $\mathrm{ut}_n$ the even-rank sequence of $K_{L,q}$ is strictly log-concave at every nontrivial interior index, confirmed through $\mathrm{ut}_6$ over $\Fq_2$.
\item \textbf{Adjoint--coadjoint incidence anticorrelation (Conjecture~\ref{v20}).}  Sampling on the incidence variety gives negative covariance between adjoint and Kirillov ranks for every nonabelian $L_P$ and zero exactly in the abelian case.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part VI: twenty conjectures on local arithmetic structure, in statement order.}
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{96}
\item \textbf{Local-sieve limit for polygonal compasses (Conjecture~\ref{w1}).}  The nearest-prime direction of the polygonal numbers $P_s$ has a limit equal to a deep local-sieve hazard, whose primes through $47$ already predict the order-level bias with correlation $0.896$.
\item \textbf{Pentagonal and pronic compasses (Conjecture~\ref{w2}).}  The pentagonal compass is right-biased in $(0.56,0.62)$ and the pronic compass left-biased in $(0.42,0.48)$, opposite directions measured at $0.583$ and $0.450$.
\item \textbf{Opposite polygonal orders (Conjecture~\ref{w3}).}  The $60$-gonal compass exceeds $0.58$ and the $69$-gonal compass falls below $0.49$, at $0.609$ and $0.468$ with both signs replicated above $10^9$.
\item \textbf{Triangular and hexagonal equality (Conjecture~\ref{w4}).}  The triangular and hexagonal compass constants are equal, by $P_6(n)=P_3(2n-1)$, agreeing to three places at about $0.536$.
\item \textbf{Square compass by root class (Conjecture~\ref{w5}).}  The nearest-prime direction of $n^2$ splits sharply by $n\bmod6$, below $0.45$ for $n\equiv1,5$ and above $0.54$ for $n\equiv0,2,3,4$.
\item \textbf{Two-sided order spectrum (Conjecture~\ref{w6}).}  As the number of sides grows the polygonal compass straddles one half, so infinitely many orders are left-biased and infinitely many right-biased.
\item \textbf{Permanent nonuniformity from length five (Conjecture~\ref{w7}).}  Every fixed-length conditional order-pattern law now has a proved limit, while nonuniformity remains open separately for every $d\ge5$, with the length-five chi-square about $2.1\times10^7$.
\item \textbf{Thirteen-fold pattern separation (Conjecture~\ref{w8}).}  At window length five the density ratio of the patterns $30241$ and $13240$ lies in $(12,15)$, measured at $13.31$ through $10^9$.
\item \textbf{Abundancy order constant (Conjecture~\ref{w9}).}  Adjacent triples of $\sigma(n)/n$ are monotone with a density in $(0.094,0.098)$, far below the independent value $1/3$, measured at $0.0958$.
\item \textbf{Totient order constant (Conjecture~\ref{w10}).}  Adjacent triples of $\phi(n)/n$ are monotone with a density in $(0.019,0.022)$, measured at $0.0202$.
\item \textbf{Five-term totient barrier (Conjecture~\ref{w11}, resolved false).}  The proposed barrier is refuted by a theorem of Martin~\cite{Martin06}, which yields strictly monotone runs of $\phi(n)/n$ of every length on positive lower density; the runs are astronomically rare, none below $4\times10^9$, whereas the abundancy analogue already fails at $n=36{,}721{,}681$.
\item \textbf{Primorial-stride order constant (Conjecture~\ref{w12}).}  Along primorial strides the abundancy order density tends to a limit in $(0.16,0.19)$, decreasing from $0.202$ at stride $30$ toward $0.177$.
\item \textbf{Dyadic completion overshoot (Conjecture~\ref{w13}).}  Raising only the dyadic exponent of an even squarefree six-divisible stride lifts the abundancy order density past $1/3$ and holds it there, isolated by two controls to the exponents rather than the prime support.
\item \textbf{Binomial-slice negative shocks (Conjecture~\ref{w14}, resolved true).}  For every $k\ge3$ the single-step $\Omega$-jump of $\binom{kn}{n}$ is unbounded below, now proved in Proposition~\ref{w:shock} by a Chinese-remainder forcing construction that loads the denominator forms with prime factors while a first-moment bound keeps the numerator forms typical.
\item \textbf{Binomial-slice positive shocks (Conjecture~\ref{w15}, resolved true).}  For every $k\ge2$ the same jump is unbounded above, so the prime-factor count is a two-sided oscillating process; the same forcing construction proves it with the two form families exchanged.
\item \textbf{Fuss--Catalan two-sided shocks (Conjecture~\ref{w16}, resolved true).}  For every $r\ge1$ the $\Omega$-jump of the Fuss--Catalan numbers is unbounded in both directions, extending the shock phenomenon beyond the binomial coefficient; Proposition~\ref{w:shock} proves both directions.
\item \textbf{Negative shock covariance (Conjecture~\ref{w17}).}  In every fixed binomial-slice, central-multinomial, and Fuss--Catalan family successive $\Omega$-jumps have a strictly negative lag-one covariance, negative in all $22$ tested families; the prime-truncated central-binomial covariance is now exactly $-\tfrac53$ at every cutoff, the predicted limit for that family.
\item \textbf{Dyadic multinomial slope (Conjecture~\ref{w18}).}  The dyadic $\Omega$-jump of $M_{k,n}$ is conjectured to have slope $-(k-1)$, with the $k=2$ case now proved by cyclotomic factorisation.
\item \textbf{Eventual sign balance (Conjecture~\ref{w19}, resolved true).}  In each binomial-slice and Fuss--Catalan family the positive- and negative-jump densities each tend to $\tfrac12$ with vanishing zero mass, now proved through the multivariate Erd\H{o}s--Kac theorem of~\cite{ELS22} applied to the exact jump identities.
\item \textbf{Boundary Euler sum (Conjecture~\ref{w20}).}  The mean prime-factor balance of the four composites bracketing a fixed prime gap equals an explicit $\ell$-adic Euler sum, matched at correlation $0.998$ across $39$ gaps.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part VII: twenty conjectures on topological invariants of geometric families, in statement order.}
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{116}
\item \textbf{Quadric-ladder ratio law (Conjecture~\ref{x1}, resolved true).}  The all-quadric Calabi--Yau Euler indices satisfy $Q_{n+1}/Q_n<8$ with strictly increasing ratios for $n\ge5$, tending to $8$; since resolved true, all three clauses proved with the sharpening $8-Q_{n+1}/Q_n\sim4/n$, Proposition~\ref{x:qladder}, the limit $8=2^3$ being the $d=2$ member of the Euler family $d^{\,d+1}$.
\item \textbf{Normalized merge monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{x2}).}  The degree-normalized Euler index $E_n/D$ strictly increases under every degree merge, while the raw version fails at $E_4(2,2,4)=1632>1476=E_4(3,4)$, the retained control.
\item \textbf{Maximal-degree merge monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{x3}).}  Every merge involving a largest degree strictly increases the unnormalized index $E_n$, in all $377{,}356$ tested merges through dimension $30$.
\item \textbf{Extremal multidegrees (Conjecture~\ref{x4}).}  The all-quadric and hypersurface configurations are the unique Euler extremes at every dimension, flagged for elevated attribution risk as likely accessible by coefficient inequalities.
\item \textbf{Dimension-wise Euler gcd law (Conjecture~\ref{x5}).}  The gcd of Calabi--Yau complete-intersection Euler characteristics at dimension $n$ equals $24/\gcd(24,n)$, doubled exactly when $n\equiv0,2\pmod8$, verified in every dimension $2$ to $30$.
\item \textbf{Five-link cap extremum (Conjecture~\ref{x6}).}  Among Fano Brieskorn $4$-tuples with cap $A\ge9$ the middle Betti number is at most $A-1$, with equality only at $(2,2,A,A)$, exact through cap $50$.
\item \textbf{Seven-link cap extremum (Conjecture~\ref{x7}).}  For $5$-tuples with cap $A\ge13$ the bound is $(A-1)(A-2)$, uniquely at $(2,2,A,A,A)$, the transition at $A=12$ peaking at $222$.
\item \textbf{Connected gcd-graph positivity (Conjecture~\ref{x8}).}  A connected gcd graph on a Brieskorn $4$-tuple forces positive middle homology, a dimension-specific law false at $N=5$, flagged for elevated attribution risk.
\item \textbf{Common-scale monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{x9}, resolved true).}  Scaling all exponents of a Brieskorn tuple by a common factor never decreases the middle Betti number, now proved in Proposition~\ref{x:ehrhart}: the scale sequence is a sum of interior Ehrhart counts of integral weighted hypersimplices, with every forward difference through order $N-1$ nonnegative.
\item \textbf{Scale convexity and log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{x10}).}  The same scale sequences are simultaneously discretely convex and log-concave; the convexity clause is now proved by the Ehrhart model, and the log-concavity clause survives an exact stress of $24{,}950{,}000$ inequalities.
\item \textbf{Hochster interval support (Conjecture~\ref{x11}).}  Every aggregated Hochster strand of a flag moment-angle complex has gapless support, though the ordinary Betti sequence of $K_{2,3}$ is not even unimodal.
\item \textbf{Hochster-strand log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{x12}).}  Every strand sequence is log-concave in the subset size, while ultra-log-concavity fails at a connected seven-vertex graph, the retained boundary.
\item \textbf{Strand Hurwitz stability (Conjecture~\ref{x13}).}  After removing the monomial factor, every strand polynomial has all zeros in the open left half-plane, verified by exact Routh--Hurwitz tests on $50{,}045$ polynomials; real-rootedness is false at four vertices.
\item \textbf{Cycle extremality at connectivity two (Conjecture~\ref{x14}).}  Among $2$-connected graphs the cycle dominates every component-strand coefficient, with coefficientwise equality only for the cycle itself.
\item \textbf{Strict component-strand log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{x15}).}  On connected noncomplete graphs the component-strand log-concavity is everywhere strict on positive triples.
\item \textbf{Parity-strand unimodality (Conjecture~\ref{x16}).}  The even and odd exterior-invariant profiles of every orientation-preserving signed permutation are unimodal, log-concavity failing at dimension $10$.
\item \textbf{Mapping-torus Betti unimodality (Conjecture~\ref{x17}).}  The full Betti sequence of the associated torus mapping torus is unimodal, log-concavity failing already at $-I_4$ with $(1,1,6,6,1,1)$.
\item \textbf{Sparse-cycle minimizer structure (Conjecture~\ref{x18}).}  Some total-Betti minimizer in every dimension $d\ge8$ uses pairwise distinct negative cycles and at most the parity-forced positive cycle, as at $(5,7)$ for $d=12$.
\item \textbf{Square-root-two compression exponent (Conjecture~\ref{x19}, resolved false).}  The claimed exponent $\sqrt2$ is refuted by the Landau floor $M_d\ge2^d/g(d)$ of Proposition~\ref{x:landau}: the true exponent is $2$, and the square-root fit was a finite-size illusion, since $\log_2g(d)\approx d/2$ throughout the tested range.
\item \textbf{Torsion budget inequality (Conjecture~\ref{x20}).}  The $2$-primary torsion generators of these mapping tori never outnumber the total rational Betti number, equivalently negative orbits are at most twice the positive orbits, with equality attained $109$ times through dimension $12$.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part VIII: twenty conjectures on shape laws for enumerative geometric invariants, in statement order.}
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{136}
\item \textbf{Strict dominance law (Conjecture~\ref{y1}).}  The signed Euler index of anticanonical hypersurfaces in products of projective spaces strictly increases along every dominance move on the ambient partition, in all $42{,}691$ tested moves through dimension $22$.
\item \textbf{Strict refinement contraction (Conjecture~\ref{y2}).}  Splitting one ambient factor always strictly decreases the signed Euler index, the mirror on ambients of Part~VII's merge monotonicity on defining equations.
\item \textbf{The $8/9$ refinement barrier (Conjecture~\ref{y3}).}  The least destructive ambient split asymptotically retains exactly eight ninths of the Euler index, with $S_{22}=0.88947$ against $8/9=0.88889$; the limit clause is flagged.
\item \textbf{Positive interaction of disjoint refinements (Conjecture~\ref{y4}).}  For dimension at least six the log Euler index is strictly supermodular on disjoint split squares, the threshold genuine with four violating squares at dimension five.
\item \textbf{Strict Betti log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{y5}).}  The Betti profiles of G\"ottsche's product are strictly log-concave in the cohomological degree for every seed $b\ge3$, covering every $\operatorname{Hilb}^n(\mathrm{K3})$, in $179{,}200$ exact Tur\'an inequalities.
\item \textbf{Cohomology-seed total positivity (Conjecture~\ref{y6}).}  Adding one seed generator concentrates the normalized profile toward the Lefschetz centre in likelihood-ratio order, in all $53{,}070$ adjacent minors.
\item \textbf{Kurtosis trichotomy (Conjecture~\ref{y7}).}  Over the proved exact mean $n$ and variance $2n/(b+2)$, the profile kurtosis follows a complete phase diagram in $b$ with a unique exceptional peak at $(6,9)$, the equality $\kappa_7(1)=\kappa_7(2)=9/2$, and limit $21/5$.
\item \textbf{Fixed-charge log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{y8}).}  At fixed displacement from middle cohomology the profiles are strictly log-concave in the number of points, making the two-parameter array log-concave along both axes.
\item \textbf{Sharp low-dimensional age unimodality (Conjecture~\ref{y9}).}  Twisted-sector age histograms of isolated cyclic Calabi--Yau quotients are unimodal in dimensions four and five, sharply: $\tfrac13(1,1,1,1,1,1)$ gives $(0,1,0,1,0)$ in dimension six; flagged for attribution risk.
\item \textbf{Gaussian bulk age law (Conjecture~\ref{y10}).}  In the random prime-order model the standardized age distribution tends to a Gaussian, a random-model statement flagged as likely accessible to classical equidistribution machinery.
\item \textbf{Local central-sector law (Conjecture~\ref{y11}).}  Individual age sectors carry lattice-Gaussian mass, with central density $\sqrt{6/(\pi d)}$, the local upgrade of the bulk law with the same flags.
\item \textbf{High-sampling restoration of unimodality (Conjecture~\ref{y12}).}  Once $r/d^3\to\infty$ the whole histogram is unimodal with probability tending to one, the exponent three deliberately exposed to refinement.
\item \textbf{Exterior torsion-entropy log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{y13}).}  The exterior-degree profile of torsion growth rates of hyperbolic toral mapping tori is log-concave, in $81{,}000$ inequalities on $16{,}200$ spectra.
\item \textbf{Equality rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{y14}).}  Interior equality in the profile forces a two-valued spectrum, in all $222$ equality cases of the exact integer corpus.
\item \textbf{Sharp binomial upper envelope (Conjecture~\ref{y15}, resolved true).}  Convexity on the product of sign simplices proves the sharp upper bound, attained by $(1,0,\ldots,0,-1)$.
\item \textbf{Two-level lower envelope (Conjecture~\ref{y16}, resolved true).}  Same-sign averaging proves that a two-level spectrum attains every lower envelope.
\item \textbf{Nullity-strand interval support (Conjecture~\ref{y17}, resolved false).}  The cycle matroid of $K_{3,3}$ has nullity-one strand $(9,0,6)$ at sizes four to six.
\item \textbf{Row-and-column log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{y18}, resolved false).}  The same strand gives the horizontal failure $0^2<9\cdot6$, while the vertical direction remains open separately.
\item \textbf{Nearest-rhombus log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{y19}, resolved false).}  A ten-edge graphic matroid has $H_{2,6}=2$, $H_{1,5}=2$, and $H_{3,7}=3$, giving $4<6$.
\item \textbf{Hurwitz stability of nullity strands (Conjecture~\ref{y20}, resolved false).}  The $K_{3,3}$ strand reduces to $9+6z^2$, whose zeros lie on the imaginary axis.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part IX: twenty conjectures on spectra and filtrations of combinatorial geometries, in statement order.}
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{156}
\item \textbf{Universal negative real roots (Conjecture~\ref{z1}).}  The $h$-polynomial of every graph associahedron has all zeros real and strictly negative, interpolating the Narayana and Eulerian endpoints, in all $878$ deposited root sets and an independent scan of all $771$ connected graphs through five vertices; flagged for attribution risk.
\item \textbf{Connected-deletion interlacing (Conjecture~\ref{z2}).}  Deleting a vertex that keeps the graph connected weakly interlaces the $h$-zeros, the recursion that would prove the root law, in $3{,}621$ deposited deletion pairs.
\item \textbf{Strict Mahler growth under edge addition (Conjecture~\ref{z3}).}  Every edge added to a connected graph strictly increases the logarithmic Mahler measure of $h_G$, in $3{,}956$ pairs with minimum increment $0.0263$.
\item \textbf{Edge addition centralizes the spectrum (Conjecture~\ref{z4}).}  Every edge addition moves the normalized even-Betti distribution of the toric variety toward middle degree in the symmetric tail order, every tested proper comparison strict.
\item \textbf{Path--star Mahler extremality (Conjecture~\ref{z5}).}  Among trees the path minimizes and the star maximizes the Mahler measure, each uniquely, the spectral shadow of known coefficientwise orders; flagged for attribution risk.
\item \textbf{One-crest component law (Conjecture~\ref{z6}).}  The component census of the age filtration of an isolated cyclic quotient with distinct weights is unimodal, in $3{,}135$ deposited profiles, distinctness being genuine: a repeated-weight control has census $(1,2,1,2,1,1,1,1)$.
\item \textbf{Early component crest (Conjecture~\ref{z7}).}  Fragmentation always peaks by half age, so the second half of the filtration only consolidates, by the age duality mechanism.
\item \textbf{Half-age connectivity (Conjecture~\ref{z8}).}  For $d\ge5$ the half-age sublevel complex is already connected, in all $2{,}905$ eligible profiles, with a disconnected repeated-weight control; flagged for attribution risk.
\item \textbf{One-crest first homology (Conjecture~\ref{z9}).}  The $\mathbf F_2$ first-Betti census of the filtration is unimodal, the simplest persistence-type law the object could satisfy.
\item \textbf{One-crest second homology (Conjecture~\ref{z10}).}  The second-Betti census is unimodal as well, and the degree bound is exact: a distinct-weight quotient with two-crest $\beta_3$ census is retained as a control.
\item \textbf{Positive-temperature log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{z11}).}  Lens-space sine-torsion partition functions are strictly log-concave across the sector index at every positive temperature, in $40{,}932$ triples with minimum curvature $0.0079$.
\item \textbf{Concavity of mean log torsion (Conjecture~\ref{z12}).}  The zero-temperature sector means are strictly concave outright, in $6{,}822$ triples with minimum curvature $0.0770$, over the exactly pinned global mean.
\item \textbf{The $\zeta(3)$ central parabola (Conjecture~\ref{z13}).}  In the iterated limit the centred sector means follow the parabola $\tfrac{3\zeta(3)}{\pi^2}(1-x^2)$, the constant forced by the proved moment identities; flagged as a limit claim and for attribution risk.
\item \textbf{Age--torsion decoupling (Conjecture~\ref{z14}).}  In the random model the age and the log-torsion sum are asymptotically jointly Gaussian and independent, strengthening the annealed age law of Conjecture~\ref{y10}; flagged as a random-model claim and for attribution risk.
\item \textbf{Negative-temperature reversal (Conjecture~\ref{z15}).}  On $-1\le s<0$ the sector shape reverses to strict log-convexity, in $20{,}466$ triples, the window calibrated by an $s=-2$ control of curvature $+0.4980$.
\item \textbf{Log-concavity of boundary entropy (Conjecture~\ref{z16}).}  The log pseudodeterminants of a simplicial sphere's boundary Laplacians are log-concave across the degree, in $262$ triples with minimum margin $51.8$.
\item \textbf{Entropy per rank decreases (Conjecture~\ref{z17}).}  The mean log eigenvalue at each degree weakly decreases with the degree, in all $392$ deposited adjacent comparisons.
\item \textbf{Simplex rigidity of equality (Conjecture~\ref{z18}).}  Any adjacent equality of entropy per rank forces the boundary of a simplex, where all equalities hold, in $130$ rigidity tests.
\item \textbf{Strict stellar growth (Conjecture~\ref{z19}).}  Stellar subdivision of a facet strictly increases every entry of the determinant profile, in $900$ comparisons with minimum increment $1.42$.
\item \textbf{Log-concave stellar increments (Conjecture~\ref{z20}).}  The entropy created by a facet subdivision is itself log-concave across the degree, in $450$ triples with minimum margin $17.8$.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part X: twenty conjectures on flux cohomology and protected index laws, in statement order.}
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{176}
\item \textbf{Dense discrete flux saturation (Conjecture~\ref{aa1}).}  Random sign flux on the torus attains the generic exterior-algebra cohomology profile with probability tending to one, exponentially fast, in $142$ of $144$ deposited and $71$ of $72$ independent trials; the random model and the exponential clause are flagged.
\item \textbf{Sparse-flux threshold at the coverage scale (Conjecture~\ref{aa2}).}  Saturation has a coarse threshold at $(\log n)/n^2$, the coverage scale of random $3$-uniform hypergraphs, the transition observed across $960$ deposited trials; the subcritical direction is now proved for every fixed $c<2$ at the coverage scale and the supercritical one is flagged.
\item \textbf{Linear-complexity sparse witnesses (Conjecture~\ref{aa3}).}  The minimum saturating support grows linearly, now proved from below in the sharp form $\mu_n\ge\lceil(n-1)/3\rceil$, with conjectured linear-hypergraph witnesses of size $n+C$, the explicit witnesses using $1,1,2,2,5,5,8,7$ triples for $n=3$ to $10$.
\item \textbf{Coefficient-law universality (Conjecture~\ref{aa4}).}  Inside the critical window the limiting saturation statistics are the same for every coefficient law, the two deposited laws tracking to $0.2125$ against $0.2125$; the corresponding statement conditional on the support is false and a five-coordinate witness is retained as a boundary control; random model flagged.
\item \textbf{Finite-field resonance power law (Conjecture~\ref{aa5}).}  Conditional on the displayed target being the true generic profile, Proposition~\ref{aa:langweil} proves the Frobenius-dependent power law by Lang--Weil.  The open content is the generic-profile identification and the geometry of the top resonance components.
\item \textbf{Central-binomial law for the complete flux (Conjecture~\ref{aa6}, resolved true).} The complete flux has cohomology totals $F_n$, a central binomial, over the rationals and $P_n$ in characteristic two, now proved from a symplectic normal form that reduces the complex to Boolean inclusion matrices.
\item \textbf{Prime torsion staircase (Conjecture~\ref{aa7}, resolved false).} The staircase clause is now proved for every prime, first $p$-torsion being exactly one $\Z/p$ at $n=4p-1$, but the elementary-abelian clause is refuted at $n=15$ by a summand $\Z/4$ in degree nine, the least dimension at which one can occur.
\item \textbf{Parity-locked torsion count (Conjecture~\ref{aa8}).}  Saturating sign flux carries exactly $(P_n-G_n)/2$ even torsion summands, forced by the proved parity calibration together with Conjecture~\ref{aa1} and the now-proved Conjecture~\ref{aa6}, while the orders fluctuate, with factors $4$, $40$, $80$ on record; retained as a corollary rather than an independent law.
\item \textbf{Coefficientwise endpoint extremality (Conjecture~\ref{aa9}).}  The all-quadric and hypersurface configurations bound every interior coefficient of the signed $\chi_y$-profile, in $7{,}124$ exact comparisons over $680$ configurations through dimension $14$.
\item \textbf{Degree-normalized merge monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{aa10}).}  Every coefficient density $a_p/D$ strictly increases under every equation merge, in $110{,}777$ exact comparisons, extending Conjecture~\ref{x2}'s Euler law to the full protected profile.
\item \textbf{Maximal-degree raw monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{aa11}).}  Merges through a maximal degree raise every interior coefficient without normalization, in $47{,}433$ exact comparisons.
\item \textbf{Real-rooted protected polynomial (Conjecture~\ref{aa12}).}  The reduced signed $\chi_y$-polynomial has only simple strictly negative real zeros, in $369$ numerically checked polynomials with smallest normalized separation $1.276\times10^{-3}$; numerical evidence flagged.
\item \textbf{Strict ultra-log-concavity (Conjecture~\ref{aa13}).}  The binomial-normalized profile is strictly log-concave, in $6{,}540$ exact inequalities, the first layer of the real-rootedness hierarchy; flagged for attribution risk beside Hodge--Riemann machinery.
\item \textbf{Degree-normalized Mahler growth (Conjecture~\ref{aa14}).}  The Mahler measure per unit degree strictly increases under every merge, in $4{,}267$ numerically checked tests, returning Conjecture~\ref{z3}'s Mahler order on the protected polynomial.
\item \textbf{Spectral-radius growth with low-dimensional rigidity (Conjecture~\ref{aa15}).}  Maximal merges never contract the root radius, with equality exactly in complex dimensions $2$, $3$, and $5$ and strict growth elsewhere, in $2{,}102$ checked tests.
\item \textbf{Signature endpoint extremality (Conjecture~\ref{aa16}).}  The all-quadric and hypersurface configurations bound the normalized signature in every even dimension, with endpoint rigidity, in $8{,}190$ exact configurations through dimension $26$.
\item \textbf{Degree-normalized signature monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{aa17}).}  The signature density $S/D$ strictly increases under every merge, in $302{,}130$ exact comparisons.
\item \textbf{Maximal-degree signature monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{aa18}).}  Merges through a maximal degree strictly raise the signature itself, in $80{,}294$ exact tests.
\item \textbf{The quadric ratio law with limit $27$ (Conjecture~\ref{aa19}, resolved true).}  Successive all-quadric signatures grow by strictly increasing ratios below $27$ converging to $27$ with correction $27/n$, exact through $n=60$ where $R_{58}=26.55045876$; since resolved true, all three clauses proved from an algebraic generating function, Proposition~\ref{aa:quad}, whose mechanism extends to every repeated block and identifies $27$ as $\cot^6(\pi/6)$.
\item \textbf{Dimension-wise signature gcd law (Conjecture~\ref{aa20}).}  The gcd of all signatures in even dimension $n$ is $2$ for $n\equiv0\pmod4$ and $2^{\max(4,1+\nu_2(n+2))}$ for $n\equiv2\pmod4$, the spike $32$ at $n=14$ confirmed, the signature sibling of Conjecture~\ref{x5}'s Euler gcd law.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part XI: twenty conjectures on spectral entropies and multibase carry fields, in statement order.}
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{196}
\item \textbf{All-order path maximality (Conjecture~\ref{ab1}).}  Among trees of a fixed order the path uniquely maximizes every R\'enyi entropy of the Laplacian density matrix simultaneously, the order-two and min-entropy cases proved, in $8{,}775$ deposited comparisons through twelve vertices and $3{,}031$ independent ones with smallest margin $2.23\times10^{-4}$; flagged for attribution risk beside the star-minimum conjecture.
\item \textbf{Leaf-pair closure law (Conjecture~\ref{ab2}).}  Closing the path between two leaves of a tree into a cycle raises the Laplacian entropy at every order, in $86{,}982$ deposited and $14{,}252$ independent comparisons with smallest margin $1.66\times10^{-3}$.
\item \textbf{Edge-subdivision law (Conjecture~\ref{ab3}).}  Subdividing any tree edge raises the Laplacian entropy at every order, in $70{,}126$ deposited and $11{,}249$ independent comparisons with the programme's most comfortable margin, $6.12\times10^{-2}$.
\item \textbf{Leaf-compression window (Conjecture~\ref{ab4}).}  Moving a leaf to a no-smaller neighbour lowers the Laplacian entropy throughout the window from order one half to two, the order-two case proved, in $15{,}385$ deposited window comparisons, while an explicit twelve-vertex tree violates the direction at order $0.1$ by $7.65\times10^{-7}$; the boundary control is retained.
\item \textbf{Unicyclic endpoint law (Conjecture~\ref{ab5}).}  Among unicyclic graphs the cycle uniquely maximizes and the star-plus-edge uniquely minimizes the Laplacian entropy at every finite order, in $6{,}230$ deposited comparisons; the exact min-entropy tie of the two four-vertex unicyclic graphs excludes the infinite order and is retained as a control.
\item \textbf{Tree one-vertex extension law (Conjecture~\ref{ab6}).}  Pendant attachment and edge subdivision raise the normalized distance signless Laplacian entropy of a tree at every order, in $168{,}168$ deposited comparisons with minimum margin $4.44\times10^{-2}$, the first entropy law posed for this spectrum.
\item \textbf{Low-order extension law (Conjecture~\ref{ab7}).}  For arbitrary connected graphs the same two extensions raise the metric entropy on the window of orders up to two, in $10{,}884$ deposited and $7{,}672$ independent comparisons, with explicit high-order violations at orders five and ten retained as controls.
\item \textbf{Star maximality (Conjecture~\ref{ab8}).}  The star uniquely maximizes the distance signless Laplacian entropy among trees at every order, reversing the Laplacian programme's path law, in $8{,}775$ deposited and $3{,}031$ independent comparisons with smallest margin $4.72\times10^{-4}$.
\item \textbf{Bipartite path minimality at the Shannon order (Conjecture~\ref{ab9}, resolved false).}  An interval-certified fourteen-vertex tree refutes the proposed path minimum with gap $3.15\times10^{-5}$.  Exhaustive enumeration of all $3{,}159$ unlabelled trees of that order finds a sharper witness with gap $9.10\times10^{-5}$.
\item \textbf{Balanced-biclique window maximality (Conjecture~\ref{ab10}).}  The balanced complete bipartite graph maximizes the metric entropy on the window from the Shannon order to order two, in $5{,}322$ deposited and $195$ independent comparisons with smallest margin $1.60\times10^{-3}$.
\item \textbf{Unicyclic cycle maximality above the Shannon order (Conjecture~\ref{ab11}).}  Among unicyclic graphs the cycle uniquely maximizes the metric entropy at every order at least one, in $2{,}245$ deposited comparisons, while a four-vertex noncycle graph wins at order $0.1$ by $6.62\times10^{-4}$; the boundary control is retained.
\item \textbf{Cross-prime carry central limit law (Conjecture~\ref{ab12}).}  The carry counts of $n+n$ at finitely many distinct odd primes are asymptotically independent Gaussians, with single-base mean and variance fixed by the proved carry-chain law, marginals and cross-correlations below $0.08$ tracked at two million integers; a limit law, flagged, with attribution risk beside the $q$-additive different-base theorem.
\item \textbf{Bounded cross-prime covariance (Conjecture~\ref{ab13}).}  The covariance of carry counts at two distinct primes stays bounded while each variance grows logarithmically, deposited covariances below $0.24$; the part's crispest analytic target.
\item \textbf{Cross-prime local limit factorization (Conjecture~\ref{ab14}).}  Joint point probabilities of the carry vector factor into products of Gaussian lattice masses at one-cell resolution; a limit law beyond any finite check, flagged as such.
\item \textbf{Modular equidistribution of carry vectors (Conjecture~\ref{ab15}).}  Carry counts at distinct primes jointly equidistribute over arbitrary fixed moduli, deposited discrepancies below $10^{-3}$ at moduli two and three; the Fourier face of cross-base mixing.
\item \textbf{Additive large deviations (Conjecture~\ref{ab16}).}  The vector of carry densities satisfies a large-deviation principle whose rate is the sum of the proved single-base rates, asserted on the interior only, away from the Diophantine boundary where the Graham entanglement lives; a limit law, flagged.
\item \textbf{Bounded mixed cumulants (Conjecture~\ref{ab17}).}  Every mixed cumulant of total order at least three across at least two primes stays bounded while marginal cumulants grow, giving a connected-correlation route to the full decoupling.
\item \textbf{Fixed-multiplier carry vectors (Conjecture~\ref{ab18}).}  The decoupling survives the richer carry automaton of $\tbinom{an}{n}$ for every fixed multiplier, deposited correlations below $0.11$ and independent ones below $0.04$ at $a=3$.
\item \textbf{Balanced factorial-ratio vectors (Conjecture~\ref{ab19}).}  Valuation vectors of every balanced integer factorial ratio decouple across primes, the Landau ratio at $p=7,11$ showing the predicted logarithmic variances with cross-correlation $-0.016$.
\item \textbf{Multibase transducer principle (Conjecture~\ref{ab20}).}  For bounded finite-state transducers in jointly multiplicatively independent bases, automaton mixing plus the absence of a common periodic factor is asserted to be the complete obstruction list for joint Gaussian decoupling; the part's most exposed statement, flagged as a programme conjecture.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part XII: twenty conjectures on subdivision spectra, spanning-tree correlation, cyclic entropy, and graphon dimension, in statement order.}

\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{216}
\item \textbf{Critical moment exponent for barycentric vertex Laplacians (Conjecture~\ref{ac1}).}  The spectral $\alpha$-moments of the refined $1$-skeleton Laplacian have exponential rate $\max\{0,\alpha\log d!-\log(d+1)!\}$, with linear growth at the critical order $\log(d+1)!/\log d!$, the exact divergence threshold that weak universality cannot see.
\item \textbf{Hodge incidence-tail hierarchy (Conjecture~\ref{ac2}).}  Across Hodge degree $k$, the critical moment exponents follow the codimension factorials $(d-k+1)!$, with degrees zero and one sharing the tail $d!$ by supersymmetry and the top degree carrying no exponential tail; the three-dimensional probe pattern is $6,6,2,1$.
\item \textbf{All-degree inclusion-uniform universality (Conjecture~\ref{ac3}).}  Every inclusion-uniform subdivision rule has universal weak Hodge limits in every degree, the common generalization of M\"arte's top-degree theorem and Knill's barycentric all-degree theorem; flagged as an attribution risk for that interpolation.
\item \textbf{Spectral-tail large deviations from simplex age (Conjecture~\ref{ac4}).}  Eigenvalues at scale $B_{d,k}^{\theta m}$ occur at exponential frequency $-\theta\log(d+1)!$, the full tail profile whose Legendre data are the critical exponents; the part's most exposed statement, with no direct tail support at reachable depth.
\item \textbf{Finite-state renormalization for barycentric Hodge limits (Conjecture~\ref{ac5}).}  A finite vector of boundary Green functions evolves under refinement by a rational map whose attracting fixed point carries all limiting Hodge laws, generalizing the known one-dimensional quadratic decimation; falsifiable by unbounded growth of the minimal boundary closure rank, on which the statement stands or falls.
\item \textbf{Complete-split extremality (Conjecture~\ref{ac6}).}  The spanning-tree correlation of a connected graph is maximized by a complete split graph with mesoscopic core of order root of $n$ over log $n$, and the maximum is $n$ minus root of $n$ log $n$ to leading order.
\item \textbf{Triangle-free bipartite extremality (Conjecture~\ref{ac7}).}  Under triangle-freeness the maximizer is a complete bipartite graph whose small side migrates at the same mesoscopic scale; the retained control is the crossover from two to three at seventeen vertices that killed the predecessor claim.
\item \textbf{Cycle minimum for bridgeless dependence (Conjecture~\ref{ac8}).}  Every bridgeless graph has spanning-tree correlation at least that of the cycle of the same order, with equality only for the cycle.
\item \textbf{Core-periphery stability at the ceiling (Conjecture~\ref{ac9}).}  Near-extremal families for the correlation ceiling must contain a mesoscopic set joined to almost the whole graph over a near-independent complement, with no structure asserted inside the core, a freedom the order-one split-bipartite gap shows is genuine.
\item \textbf{Complete-multipartite reduction (Conjecture~\ref{ac10}).}  Among complete multipartite graphs the correlation maximizer is always one macroscopic part plus singletons, collapsing the partition optimization to the split family; verified exhaustively through order forty-five.
\item \textbf{Localization of near-minimizers on prime cycles (Conjecture~\ref{ac11}).}  Mesoscopic sequences attaining the proved half-log-two doubling floor asymptotically must, after affine automorphisms, concentrate on cyclic intervals of length little-o of $p$: the inverse theorem to the 2026 prime-field entropy-power inequality.
\item \textbf{Gaussian rigidity after localization (Conjecture~\ref{ac12}).}  Under localization and a uniform moment bound, near-minimizing lifts obey a central limit theorem with entropic convergence; the moment hypothesis is load-bearing, since unrestricted continuous rigidity is known to fail.
\item \textbf{Heat-kernel wrap crossover (Conjecture~\ref{ac13}, resolved false).}  The claimed universal heat-kernel wrap limit is refuted by a mesoscopic jump family satisfying every stated hypothesis whose limit carries an extra compound-Poisson factor; a Lindeberg condition at the modular scale is the recorded repair.
\item \textbf{Fixed-$m$ entropy-power law on prime cycles (Conjecture~\ref{ac14}).}  For every fixed number of summands the mesoscopic entropy gain is at least half the logarithm of the count, with Gaussian rigidity at equality; flagged as an attribution risk, since the integer-lattice analogue is a recent theorem and only the modular content is new.
\item \textbf{Uniform pre-wrap entropy monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{ac15}).}  Log-concave cyclic laws obey the entropic central-limit increment law uniformly in the number of summands all the way to the wrap scale, with the deposited failure onset near wrap ratio one percent as the retained boundary control.
\item \textbf{Exact-dimensional entropy identity (Conjecture~\ref{ac16}).}  For random-free graphons whose row measure is exact-dimensional with local doubling, sampled-graph entropy per $n\log n$ converges exactly to the dimension.
\item \textbf{Multifractal average-dimension law (Conjecture~\ref{ac17}).}  Under an explicit $L^1$ convergence of local dimensions, the entropy coefficient is the mean pointwise information dimension, neither a Minkowski dimension nor an essential supremum.
\item \textbf{Second-order graph entropy constant (Conjecture~\ref{ac18}).}  Smooth row manifolds admit a finite linear-order entropy constant, which the threshold-graphon control proves cannot be the quantization constant of the row space.
\item \textbf{Equipartition for random-free samples (Conjecture~\ref{ac19}).}  The sample information content concentrates at the leading scale: almost every sampled graph lies in a typical set of the predicted exponential size, a Shannon--McMillan law for graphon sampling.
\item \textbf{Sparse-observation dimension law (Conjecture~\ref{ac20}).}  Under Bernoulli edge-masking with a growing probe count, conditional entropy scales as $n$ times the logarithm of the probe count times the mean dimension, quantifying information decay under missing data.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Part XIII: nineteen conjectures on flux torsion, quantum merge monotonicity, and effective string geometry, in statement order.}

\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{236}
\item \textbf{Odd-degree discrete saturation (Conjecture~\ref{ad1}).}  Sign-valued random $r$-forms attain the generic rational cohomology profile with exponentially small failure probability for every odd degree, lifting the three-form law of Part~X to the full family; sixty deposited and twenty-four independent degree-five trials all saturate.
\item \textbf{Odd coverage-scale threshold (Conjecture~\ref{ad2}).}  Sparse random odd flux saturates exactly at the isolated-vertex scale of random $r$-uniform hypergraphs, with the suspension dimensions excluded by the essentiality hypothesis; the subcritical direction is now proved and the supercritical one is flagged.
\item \textbf{Torsion shape law for the complete flux (Conjecture~\ref{ad3}).} The degreewise prime-torsion multiplicities of the complete flux are strictly log-concave in the interior, with the shifted duality and the interval support, now identified as the full range $2p+1$ to $n-2p+2$, both proved; log-concavity is verified for every prime up to twenty-three through dimension one hundred fifty.
\item \textbf{Post-onset torsion persistence (Conjecture~\ref{ad4}, resolved true).} Once $p$-torsion appears at dimension $4p-1$ its total strictly grows forever, now proved for every prime from the normal form; the two-primary totals run $1,2,10,20,66,132$.
\item \textbf{Normalized mirror-map contraction (Conjecture~\ref{ad5}).}  In the scale-free normalization by the discriminant scale, every coefficient of the mirror map contracts under equation merges from dimension three on, with the dimension-three order-two equalities as the exact boundary; five hundred eighty-eight independent comparisons pass.
\item \textbf{Gopakumar--Vafa amplification (Conjecture~\ref{ad6}).}  Across every merge edge of the five one-parameter threefolds, the ratio of genus-zero curve counts grows strictly with the degree, an ordering of geometries by quantum growth refined degree by degree.
\item \textbf{Monotone conifold approach (Conjecture~\ref{ad7}).}  The genus-zero invariants of each of the five threefolds are strictly log-convex from degree one, with ratios climbing monotonically to the conifold growth constant.
\item \textbf{Merge monotonicity of the conifold constant (Conjecture~\ref{ad8}).}  The conifold point in the flat coordinate strictly decreases under every merge, so consolidation strictly raises the quantum growth constant, the transcendental image of the proved algebraic ordering.
\item \textbf{Conifold-normalized Yukawa monotonicity (Conjecture~\ref{ad9}).}  At equal fractions of the respective conifold radii, the Yukawa coupling normalized by the classical intersection number strictly increases under merges, with the raw instanton comparison failing everywhere as the retained control.
\item \textbf{Quadratic wall complexity (Conjecture~\ref{ad10}).}  The number of numerical walls meeting a compact region grows at most quadratically in the charge norm.  A support-property count is only polynomial and the quadratic exponent requires extra Picard-rank-one geometry.
\item \textbf{Sharp Gamma for Brill--Noether stability (Conjecture~\ref{ad11}).}  The Gamma-constant produced by the Brill--Noether construction of stability conditions is exactly optimal on its ray, its topological floor computed by the proved Chern calibration as five sixths down to one third across the five threefold configurations.
\item \textbf{Tame wall atlas (Conjecture~\ref{ad12}).}  In a fixed effective analytic-exponential presentation, the aggregate wall arrangement has polynomial formula format and a polynomial-size compatible stratification up to every charge cutoff.
\item \textbf{Polynomial conditioning of Ricci-flat metrics (Conjecture~\ref{ad13}).}  All curvature derivatives, the diameter and the regularity scale of Ricci-flat metrics are polynomially controlled in a power-equivalence class of discriminant gauges.
\item \textbf{Discriminant-effective balanced convergence (Conjecture~\ref{ad14}).}  Balanced metrics converge at the classical rate uniformly in the family once the level exceeds a power of the inverse discriminant gauge.
\item \textbf{Discriminant-effective Hermitian--Yang--Mills convergence (Conjecture~\ref{ad15}).}  Balanced-bundle approximations carry a polynomial condition number in the minimum of a discriminant gauge and a quantitative stability margin.
\item \textbf{Polynomial matter spectral gap (Conjecture~\ref{ad16}).}  The first positive bundle-valued Dolbeault eigenvalue is bounded below by a power of the pair gauge wherever the matter cohomology is constant.
\item \textbf{Certified heterotic Yukawa convergence (Conjecture~\ref{ad17}).}  Numerically computed physical Yukawa couplings admit a-priori error certificates polynomial in the pair and coupling-zero gauge classes.
\item \textbf{Saturation-index special-Lagrangian realization (Conjecture~\ref{ad18}).}  At infinite-distance degenerations, every extremal light charge has a calibrated representative after multiplication by a divisor of the saturation exponent of the monodromy-orbit lattice.
\item \textbf{LMHS-controlled Laplace towers (Conjecture~\ref{ad19}).}  The vanishing Ricci-flat Laplace spectrum has finitely many exhaustive towers.  Their rational exponents come from the integral limiting mixed Hodge structure and skeleton combinatorics, while the limiting operators may retain metric data.
\end{enumerate}







\begin{abstract}
We state two hundred fifty-five conjectures in thirteen parts.  The twenty-five conjectures of Part~I are each derived from the
local--global random model of the primes:
Cram\'er's model corrected by Hardy--Littlewood singular series, with
Bateman--Horn as the organizing framework and Borel--Cantelli accounting
for sparse events.  Each statement is calibrated to the strongest form the
heuristic supports.  Every computable constant is
computed from its definition, with local admissibility checked for
every constant computed; every statement was tested against exact
counts (probable-prime counts, so labelled, where the integers
involved exceed the deterministic certification range), with
success measured by the \emph{shape} of agreement (predicted constant,
square-root residuals, no drift) rather than by the size of the range
searched.  The contributions include: a prime-power contamination
calculus for pattern races---twin races modulo $5$ and $8$ driven by the
pairs $(q^2-2,q^2)$, cousin, sexy-pair, and triplet predictions derived
from the calculus before testing, a Goldbach lane race with a verified
internal null lane, a Stern-representation lane race whose null classes
are provable by direct congruence, and an algebraic null control; the
measured sub-diffusivity of balanced races (universal negative step
correlations tied to the Lemke Oliver--Soundararajan repulsion);
canonical ordering deficits for least primes in progressions and for
least Goldbach summands; derived covariance kernels for moving-window
residual fields, with the diagonal deficit reduced to a pinned
singular-series average whose growth already exceeds the single-prime
Montgomery--Soundararajan term; distribution laws for the constants of
the quadratic-pair and cubic-shift families, with exact mean-value
lemmas; an entanglement-aware local-global law for $n^2+2^n$ with an
exact-rational CRT factorization; a boundary trichotomy for polynomial
ladders; a multibase subtorus law for Fermat quotients; and
singular-series waiting-time refinements for first prime gaps.  Every
statement was checked against the literature by an independent search,
with novelty labelled conservatively: statements already known in
essence are attributed and kept \emph{inside} the conjecture whose
content they calibrate, since those benchmarks are what make the derived
constants credible.  Every falsifiable-by-instance statement was then
stress-tested computationally well beyond its original range, and every
constant was recomputed independently from its definition.  Part~II adds
twenty-five structural conjectures in five programmes---connected
prime-pattern fields, arithmetic first-arrival fields and class groups,
finite logarithms and algebraic tori, arboreal arithmetic dynamics, and
adelic factorization processes---each introducing a canonical operator,
process, invariant, classification, or phase boundary, with its mechanism,
nearest literature boundary, first decisive theorem, and failure mode.
  Part~III adds six conjectures across fields: a relative pretentious inverse principle for polynomial arguments, a threshold purification law for monotone vector paths, an entropy--dimension law for random-free graphons, a Hessian principle for microcanonical graph phases, a sharp stability law for entropy idempotence on finite abelian groups, and a convolution-entropy rigidity for stationary processes.  Part~IV (Conjectures~57--76) adds twenty conjectures on a single further object, Rossmann's class-counting polynomial of graphical Lie algebras: rigidity and reconstruction laws for the polynomial and its binary specialization, recovery of the subgraph-component polynomial and of domination invariants, a strict log-concavity ladder with proven failure boundaries, and extremal laws for trees, each tested exhaustively at small order, on adversarial random holdouts, and at every known collision class of the polynomial.  Two of the twenty, the pseudoforest one-field rigidity claim and the interval-support claim, have since been refuted, by an explicit seven-vertex pair and a connected eight-vertex graph, and are recorded as resolved false with their counterexamples.  Part~V (Conjectures~77--96) adds a second algebraic family, the nilpotent pattern Lie algebras of finite posets, and studies two rank-weight enumerators of the bracket tensor, the adjoint and the Kirillov enumerators: determination of structural and coadjoint invariants by the adjoint enumerator, characteristic and field-monotonicity behaviour, a positive unimodal centre-one incidence quotient, and reverse determination, saturation, and a breadth bound for Hasse-forest posets, fifteen of them supported throughout the tested corpus, three stated in greater generality than the computations reach, and two since refuted by explicit seven-point posets and recorded as resolved false, all reverified on an independently generated poset corpus over a proved calibration identity.  Part~VI (Conjectures~97--116) returns to the local--global aesthetic of Part~I on new objects: prime compasses at polynomial centres matched to local-sieve hazards, order patterns of the radical ratio with every fixed conditional density now proved to exist and nonuniformity open beyond length four, order constants of abundancy and totient ratios, unbounded prime-factor shocks in combinatorial sequences resting on seven proved propositions, and a local--global Euler sum for the prime-factor anatomy of prime-gap boundaries, one of the twenty since resolved false by a theorem of G.~Martin, the three two-sided shock laws and the sign-balance law since proved, and six stated as measured bands rather than derived constants.  Part~VII (Conjectures~117--136) crosses into algebraic topology on four canonical geometric families: Euler indices of Calabi--Yau complete intersections ending in an exact dimension-wise gcd law, middle Betti numbers of Brieskorn--Pham links under degree caps and common scaling, Hochster strands of flag moment-angle complexes with interval, log-concavity, and Hurwitz-stability shape laws, and torus mapping tori with signed monodromy over proved signed-orbit and normalised-merge calibrations, two of the twenty asserting limits beyond the tested range and two flagged for elevated attribution risk, the quadric ratio law and normalised merge law since proved in full, the compression-exponent law since resolved false with true exponent two, and the common-scale law since proved by an Ehrhart model.  Part~VIII (Conjectures~137--156) continues into enumerative topology with five shape-law programmes: Euler indices of anticanonical hypersurfaces in products of projective spaces under dominance and refinement, Betti profiles of Hilbert schemes over a proved pair of exact moment identities, twisted-sector age histograms of cyclic Calabi--Yau quotients, exterior torsion-entropy profiles of hyperbolic toral automorphisms, and bigraded Hochster tables of simple binary matroids, with two limit clauses and three random-model statements flagged and one attribution risk marked, two statements since proved by convexity, and four since refuted by exact graphic certificates.
